Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

A boy named Ayrat lives on planet AMI-1511. Each inhabitant of this planet has a talent. Specifically, Ayrat loves running, moreover, just running is not enough for him. He is dreaming of making running a real art.

First, he wants to construct the running track with coating t. On planet AMI-1511 the coating of the track is the sequence of colored blocks, where each block is denoted as the small English letter. Therefore, every coating can be treated as a string.

Unfortunately, blocks aren't freely sold to non-business customers, but Ayrat found an infinite number of coatings s. Also, he has scissors and glue. Ayrat is going to buy some coatings s, then cut out from each of them exactly one continuous piece (substring) and glue it to the end of his track coating. Moreover, he may choose to flip this block before glueing it. Ayrat want's to know the minimum number of coating s he needs to buy in order to get the coating t for his running track. Of course, he also want's to know some way to achieve the answer.


-----Input-----

First line of the input contains the string s — the coating that is present in the shop. Second line contains the string t — the coating Ayrat wants to obtain. Both strings are non-empty, consist of only small English letters and their length doesn't exceed 2100.


-----Output-----

The first line should contain the minimum needed number of coatings n or -1 if it's impossible to create the desired coating.

If the answer is not -1, then the following n lines should contain two integers x_{i} and y_{i} — numbers of ending blocks in the corresponding piece. If x_{i} ≤ y_{i} then this piece is used in the regular order, and if x_{i} > y_{i} piece is used in the reversed order. Print the pieces in the order they should be glued to get the string t.


-----Examples-----
Input
abc
cbaabc

Output
2
3 1
1 3

Input
aaabrytaaa
ayrat

Output
3
1 1
6 5
8 7

Input
ami
no

Output
-1



-----Note-----

In the first sample string "cbaabc" = "cba" + "abc".

In the second sample: "ayrat" = "a" + "yr" + "at".

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a coating string `s`
2. We can cut out any substring from `s` and glue it to the end of our target string `t`
3. We can choose to reverse the substring before gluing
4. We want to find the minimum number of such operations to create string `t`

## Key Insights

1. If `t` contains a substring that is not a substring of `s`, then it's impossible (return -1)
2. For each position in `t`, we need to determine which substring from `s` we should use
3. We want to minimize the total number of substrings used

## Strategy

1. Check if it's possible: verify that every character in `t` appears in `s`
2. For each prefix of `t`, we can try to match it with a substring from `s`, possibly reversed
3. We need to find the optimal way to build `t` by concatenating substrings from `s`

## Algorithm

1. First, check if every character in `t` exists in `s`. If not, return -1.
2. Use dynamic programming to find the minimum number of substrings needed:
   - `dp[i]` = minimum number of substrings to form the first `i` characters of `t`
   - For each position, try all possible substrings ending at that position
3. For each substring we use, we track which indices in `s` we're taking from

## Implementation

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if every character in t exists in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    n = len(t)
    # dp[i] = minimum number of substrings to form first i characters of t
    # parent[i] = (last_length, start_idx, end_idx) for the last substring
    dp = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
    parent = [-1] * (n + 1)  # store (start, end) for the last substring
    
    dp[0] = 0
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        # Try all possible lengths of the last substring
        for j in range(1, i + 1):
            # Last substring is from position i-j to i-1 in t
            substring = t[i-j:i]
            
            # Check if this substring exists in s (in normal order or reversed)
            found = False
            # Try normal order first
            for start in range(len(s)):
                if s[start:start+len(substring)] == substring:
                    dp[i] = min(dp[i], dp[i-j] + 1)
                    # Store the information
                    if dp[i] == dp[i-j] + 1:
                        parent[i] = (start, start + len(substring) - 1)
                    found = True
                    break
            
            if found:
                continue
                
            # Try reversed order
            reverse_substring = substring[::-1]
            for start in range(len(s)):
                if s[start:start+len(reverse_substring)] == reverse_substring:
                    dp[i] = min(dp[i], dp[i-j] + 1)
                    if dp[i] == dp[i-j] + 1:
                        parent[i] = (start, start + len(reverse_substring) - 1)
                    found = True
                    break
    
    if dp[n] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Reconstruct the solution
    result = []
    i = n
    while i > 0:
        start, end = parent[i]
        # The substring is from start to end in s
        # If start > end, it's reversed
        if start > end:
            result.append((end + 1, start))  # Convert to 1-indexed
        else:
            result.append((start + 1, end + 1))  # Convert to 1-indexed
        i -= len(t[i - len(t[i-1]):i])  # This is incorrect, let me fix the reconstruction
    
    # Let me fix the reconstruction logic
    result = []
    i = n
    while i > 0:
        # Find the length of the last segment
        for length in range(1, i + 1):
            if dp[i] == dp[i-length] + 1:
                segment = t[i-length:i]
                # Find if segment exists in normal or reversed order in s
                found_normal = False
                found_reversed = False
                
                # Check normal
                for start in range(len(s) - len(segment) + 1):
                    if s[start:start+len(segment)] == segment:
                        found_normal = True
                        result.append((start + 1, start + len(segment)))
                        break
                
                if found_normal:
                    break
                else:
                    # Check reversed
                    for start in range(len(s) - len(segment) + 1):
                        if s[start:start+len(segment)] == segment[::-1]:
                            found_reversed = True
                            result.append((start + 1, start + len(segment)))
                            break
                    if found_reversed:
                        break
        i -= length
    
    # The above reconstruction is messy. Let me rewrite with a cleaner approach
    
    # Actually, let me use a simpler approach: backtracking from the end
    result = []
    i = n
    while i > 0:
        found = False
        for length in range(1, i + 1):
            segment = t[i-length:i]
            # Check normal order
            for start in range(len(s) - len(segment) + 1):
                if s[start:start+len(segment)] == segment:
                    result.append((start + 1, start + len(segment)))
                    i -= length
                    found = True
                    break
            if found:
                break
            # Check reversed order
            for start in range(len(s) - len(segment) + 1):
                if s[start:start+len(segment)] == segment[::-1]:
                    result.append((start + 1, start + len(segment)))
                    i -= length
                    found = True
                    break
        if not found:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Reverse the result since we built it backwards
    result.reverse()
    
    # Print results
    print(dp[n])
    for start, end in result:
        if start > end:
            print(end, start)
        else:
            print(start, end)
```

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if every character in t exists in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    n = len(t)
    # dp[i] = minimum number of substrings to form first i characters of t
    dp = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
    parent = [-1] * (n + 1)  # parent[i] = (start, end) for the last substring
    
    dp[0] = 0
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        # Try all possible lengths of the last substring
        for j in range(1, i + 1):
            segment = t[i-j:i]
            segment_len = len(segment)
            
            # Try to find segment in normal order
            found = False
            for start in range(len(s) - segment_len + 1):
                if s[start:start+segment_len] == segment:
                    if dp[i-j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                        dp[i] = dp[i-j] + 1
                        parent[i] = (start, start + segment_len - 1)
                    found = True
                    break
            
            if found:
                continue
                
            # Try to find segment in reversed order
            reversed_segment = segment[::-1]
            for start in range(len(s) - segment_len + 1):
                if s[start:start+segment_len] == reversed_segment:
                    if dp[i-j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                        dp[i] = dp[i-j] + 1
                        parent[i] = (start, start + segment_len - 1)
                    found = True
                    break
    
    if dp[n] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Reconstruct the solution
    result = []
    i = n
    while i > 0:
        segment_len = 0
        # Find the correct length of the last segment
        for j in range(1, i + 1):
            if dp[i] == dp[i-j] + 1:
                segment_len = j
                break
        
        segment = t[i-segment_len:i]
        
        # Determine if it's normal or reversed
        start, end = parent[i]
        
        if start > end:
            # This means we used reversed order, so the original segment was reversed
            # We need to show the original indices
            result.append((end + 1, start))
        else:
            result.append((start + 1, end + 1))
        
        i -= segment_len
    
    # Reverse result to get correct order
    result.reverse()
    
    print(dp[n])
    for start, end in result:
        print(start, end)
```

                    found = True

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a coating string `s`
2. We need to create target string `t` by taking substrings from `s` and either using them as-is or reversed
3. We want to minimize the number of substrings we take
4. Each substring is defined by its start and end position (1-indexed)

## Key Insights

1. If `t` contains any character not present in `s`, it's impossible → return -1
2. We need to find the minimum number of segments from `s` to build `t`
3. For each position in `t`, we need to find a substring in `s` that matches either:
   - The current character (using forward direction)
   - The reverse of the current character (using backward direction)

## Strategy

This is essentially a dynamic programming problem where we want to build the string `t` character by character.

1. First check if all characters in `t` exist in `s`. If not, return -1.
2. Use DP where `dp[i]` represents the minimum number of pieces needed to build the first `i` characters of `t`.
3. For each position, try all possible substrings from `s` that can match the current part of `t`.

However, since the string length can be up to 2100, we need an efficient approach.

Let me think of a simpler greedy approach:
- We can match characters one by one
- For each position in `t`, we need to find a substring from `s` that matches either forward or backward
- We want to maximize the length of each substring to minimize the total count

Actually, let me approach this differently:

## Better Approach

Since we can take any substring from `s`, we need to check if `t` can be constructed. For each segment of `t`, we need to find if it appears in `s` or in reverse.

But we want to minimize the number of segments. So we should try to match longer substrings whenever possible.

Algorithm:
1. Check if all characters in `t` exist in `s`. If not, return -1.
2. Use dynamic programming where `dp[i]` is the minimum number of segments to form `t[0:i]`.
3. For each position `i`, try all possible end positions of previous segments and check if the remaining part of `t` matches a substring of `s` in forward or reverse order.

Wait, this might be too complex. Let me think simpler:

Since we can take any substring from `s`, and we can reverse it, we can match any substring that appears in `s` or in reverse of `s`.

The key insight is that we need to find the minimum number of pieces such that the concatenation of pieces (in order) forms `t`, where each piece is a substring of `s` or its reverse.

## Final Approach

1. First check if all characters in `t` exist in `s`. If any character is missing, return -1.
2. Use dynamic programming:
   - `dp[i]` = minimum number of pieces to form `t[0:i]`
   - For each position `i`, try all possible previous positions `j` (where `j < i`)
   - The remaining substring `t[j:i]` must be a substring of `s` or the reverse of a substring of `s`
3. For each possible substring in `t`, check if it appears in `s` or in reverse of `s`.

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    for char in t:
        if char not in s:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Precompute all substrings of s and their reversed versions
    # For efficiency, we'll create a set of all possible substrings
    substrings = set()
    reverse_substrings = set()
    
    n = len(s)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i, n):
            substrings.add(s[i:j+1])
            reverse_substrings.add(s[i:j+1][::-1])
    
    # Combine both sets - a substring or its reverse is valid
    valid_substrings = substrings | reverse_substrings
    
    # DP to find minimum number of segments
    # dp[i] = (min_pieces, path) for first i characters of t
    dp = [float('inf')] * (len(t) + 1)
    dp[0] = 0
    
    # parent[i] stores the previous position and the segment details
    parent = [None] * (len(t) + 1)
    
    for i in range(1, len(t) + 1):
        for j in range(i):
            # Try to extend from position j to i
            segment = t[j:i]
            if segment in valid_substrings:
                if dp[j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                    dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
                    parent[i] = (j, segment)
    
    if dp[len(t)] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Reconstruct the solution
    result = []
    pos = len(t)
    while pos > 0:
        j, segment = parent[pos]
        # Find the original substring in s and its bounds
        found = False
        for i in range(len(s)):
            for k in range(i, len(s)):
                sub = s[i:k+1]
                if sub == segment or sub[::-1] == segment:
                    # Find if this substring is in the original or reversed
                    if sub == segment:
                        result.append((i+1, k+1))  # 1-indexed
                    else:  # sub[::-1] == segment
                        result.append((k+1, i+1))  # reversed, so start > end
                    found = True
                    break
            if found:
                break
        pos = j
    
    result.reverse()
    print(dp[len(t)])
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)

solve()
```

Wait, this solution might be too slow for strings of length 2100. Let me optimize it.

Actually, let me reconsider. The naive approach of checking every substring will be O(n^3) which is too slow.

We need to precompute all valid substrings efficiently, but we also need to find the actual positions in s.

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    for char in t:
        if char not in s:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Precompute the set of all possible substrings from s and their reverses
    valid_substrings = set()
    n = len(s)
    
    # For efficiency, we'll create a mapping from substring to its possible positions
    substring_positions = {}
    
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i, n):
            substr = s[i:j+1]
            reverse_substr = substr[::-1]
            valid_substrings.add(substr)
            valid_substrings.add(reverse_substr)
            
            if substr not in substring_positions:
                substring_positions[substr] = []
            if reverse_substr not in substring_positions:
                substring_positions[reverse_substr] = []
            # Store the actual positions for this substring
            # We might need to store the first occurrence or all occurrences
            # For now, we just need to know that it exists
    
    # DP to find minimum number of segments
    m = len(t)
    dp = [float('inf')] * (m + 1)
    dp[0] = 0
    
    # parent[i] = (prev_position, substring_used, start_position, end_position)
    parent = [None] * (m + 1)
    
    for i in range(1, m + 1):
        for j in range(i):
            segment = t[j:i]
            if segment in valid_substrings:
                if dp[j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                    dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
                    parent[i] = (j, segment)
    
    if dp[m] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Reconstruct the solution
    result = []
    pos = m
    while pos > 0:
        prev_pos, segment = parent[pos]
        # Find the actual positions in s for this segment
        found = False
        for i in range(len(s)):
            for k in range(i, len(s)):
                substr = s[i:k+1]
                if substr == segment:
                    result.append((i+1, k+1))  # 1-indexed, regular order
                    found = True
                    break
            if found:
                break
        
        if not found:
            # Try reversed
            for i in range(len(s)):
                for k in range(i, len(s)):
                    substr = s[i:k+1]
                    rev = substr[::-1]
                    if rev == segment:
                        result.append((k+1, i+1))  # 1-indexed, reversed order
                        found = True
                        break
                if found:
                    break
        
        pos = prev_pos
    
    result.reverse()
    print(dp[m])
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)

solve()
```

```python
s = input().strip()
t = input().strip()

# Check if all characters in t exist in s
for char in t:
    if char not in s:
        print(-1)
        exit()

# Precompute all possible substrings and their positions
from collections import defaultdict
valid_substrings = set()
substring_positions = defaultdict(list)

n = len(s)
for i in range(n):
    for j in range(i, n):
        substr = s[i:j+1]
        valid_substrings.add(substr)
        valid_substrings.add(substr[::-1])
        substring_positions[substr].append((i, j))
        substring_positions[substr[::-1]].append((j, i))  # reversed position

# DP to find minimum number of segments
m = len(t)
dp = [float('inf')] * (m + 1)
dp[0] = 0

parent = [None] * (m + 1)

for i in range(1, m + 1):
    for j in range(i):
        segment = t[j:i]
        if segment in valid_substrings:
            if dp[j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
                parent[i] = (j, segment)

if dp[m] == float('inf'):
    print(-1)
else:
    # Reconstruct the solution
    result = []
    pos = m
    while pos > 0:
        j, segment = parent[pos]
        
        # Find the actual (start, end) positions in s
        found = False
        # Check if segment appears in forward
        for start, end in substring_positions[segment]:
            if segment == s[start:end+1]:
                result.append((start+1, end+1))  # 1-indexed
                found = True
                break
                
        if not found:
            # Check if segment appears in reverse
            for start, end in substring_positions[segment]:
                if segment == s[start:end+1][::-1]:
                    result.append((end+1, start+1))  # 1-indexed, reversed
                    found = True
                    break
        
        pos = j
    
    result.reverse()
    print(dp[m])
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)
```

Submitted Code

View Extracted Code 
s = input().strip()
t = input().strip()

# Check if all characters in t exist in s
for char in t:
    if char not in s:
        print(-1)
        exit()

# Precompute all possible substrings and their positions
from collections import defaultdict
valid_substrings = set()
substring_positions = defaultdict(list)

n = len(s)
for i in range(n):
    for j in range(i, n):
        substr = s[i:j+1]
        valid_substrings.add(substr)
        valid_substrings.add(substr[::-1])
        substring_positions[substr].append((i, j))
        substring_positions[substr[::-1]].append((j, i))  # reversed position

# DP to find minimum number of segments
m = len(t)
dp = [float('inf')] * (m + 1)
dp[0] = 0

parent = [None] * (m + 1)

for i in range(1, m + 1):
    for j in range(i):
        segment = t[j:i]
        if segment in valid_substrings:
            if dp[j] + 1 < dp[i]:
                dp[i] = dp[j] + 1
                parent[i] = (j, segment)

if dp[m] == float('inf'):
    print(-1)
else:
    # Reconstruct the solution
    result = []
    pos = m
    while pos > 0:
        j, segment = parent[pos]
        
        # Find the actual (start, end) positions in s
        found = False
        # Check if segment appears in forward
        for start, end in substring_positions[segment]:
            if segment == s[start:end+1]:
                result.append((start+1, end+1))  # 1-indexed
                found = True
                break
                
        if not found:
            # Check if segment appears in reverse
            for start, end in substring_positions[segment]:
                if segment == s[start:end+1][::-1]:
                    result.append((end+1, start+1))  # 1-indexed, reversed
                    found = True
                    break
        
        pos = j
    
    result.reverse()
    print(dp[m])
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

A boy named Ayrat lives on planet AMI-1511. Each inhabitant of this planet has a talent. Specifically, Ayrat loves running, moreover, just running is not enough for him. He is dreaming of making running a real art.

First, he wants to construct the running track with coating t. On planet AMI-1511 the coating of the track is the sequence of colored blocks, where each block is denoted as the small English letter. Therefore, every coating can be treated as a string.

Unfortunately, blocks aren't freely sold to non-business customers, but Ayrat found an infinite number of coatings s. Also, he has scissors and glue. Ayrat is going to buy some coatings s, then cut out from each of them exactly one continuous piece (substring) and glue it to the end of his track coating. Moreover, he may choose to flip this block before glueing it. Ayrat want's to know the minimum number of coating s he needs to buy in order to get the coating t for his running track. Of course, he also want's to know some way to achieve the answer.


-----Input-----

First line of the input contains the string s — the coating that is present in the shop. Second line contains the string t — the coating Ayrat wants to obtain. Both strings are non-empty, consist of only small English letters and their length doesn't exceed 2100.


-----Output-----

The first line should contain the minimum needed number of coatings n or -1 if it's impossible to create the desired coating.

If the answer is not -1, then the following n lines should contain two integers x_{i} and y_{i} — numbers of ending blocks in the corresponding piece. If x_{i} ≤ y_{i} then this piece is used in the regular order, and if x_{i} > y_{i} piece is used in the reversed order. Print the pieces in the order they should be glued to get the string t.


-----Examples-----
Input
abc
cbaabc

Output
2
3 1
1 3

Input
aaabrytaaa
ayrat

Output
3
1 1
6 5
8 7

Input
ami
no

Output
-1



-----Note-----

In the first sample string "cbaabc" = "cba" + "abc".

In the second sample: "ayrat" = "a" + "yr" + "at".

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a coating string `s`
2. We can cut out any substring from `s` and either use it as-is or flipped (reversed)
3. We want to construct target string `t` by concatenating these substrings (with optional reversal)
4. We need to find the minimum number of such operations

## Key Insights

1. If `t` contains a character that doesn't appear in `s`, it's impossible → return -1
2. We need to break `t` into segments, each segment being a substring of `s` (possibly reversed)
3. For each segment, we want to minimize the total count
4. We can use dynamic programming or greedy approach to find the optimal segmentation

## Algorithm Approach

1. **Check feasibility**: If any character in `t` isn't in `s`, return -1
2. **Greedy approach**: Try to extend the current segment as much as possible until we can't extend further
3. **Pattern matching**: For each position in `t`, find the longest prefix of `s` that matches a suffix of the current segment
4. **Check reversals**: For each potential segment, check if it can be obtained by either direct match or reverse match

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # We'll use a greedy approach to build t from s segments
    result = []
    i = 0  # current position in target string t
    
    while i < len(t):
        # Try to find the longest prefix of s that matches a suffix of current position
        # We'll try to extend as much as possible from current position i
        
        found = False
        # Try to match with original s or reversed s
        for start in range(len(s)):
            for end in range(start, len(s)):
                segment = s[start:end+1]
                # Check if this segment can match starting at position i
                # Case 1: direct match
                if segment == t[i:i+len(segment)]:
                    result.append((start + 1, end + 1))  # 1-indexed
                    i += len(segment)
                    found = True
                    break
                
                # Case 2: reversed match
                reversed_segment = segment[::-1]
                if reversed_segment == t[i:i+len(segment)]:
                    result.append((start + 1, end + 1))  # 1-indexed, reversed
                    i += len(segment)
                    found = True
                    break
            if found:
                break
        
        if not found:
            # If no segment matches, try if any single character works
            if i < len(t):
                # Try single character
                char = t[i]
                for j in range(len(s)):
                    if s[j] == char:
                        result.append((j + 1, j + 1))  # single character
                        i += 1
                        break
                else:
                    # This shouldn't happen as we already checked character existence
                    print(-1)
                    return
    
    print(len(result))
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)

solve()
```

Wait, I think my greedy approach might not be optimal. Let me reconsider with a more systematic approach.

Actually, let me implement a cleaner solution with proper greedy matching:

```python
s = input().strip()
t = input().strip()

# Check if all characters in t exist in s
s_set = set(s)
for char in t:
    if char not in s_set:
        print(-1)
        exit()

# Greedy approach: try to match as long as possible from each position
result = []
i = 0

while i < len(t):
    found = False
    # Try all possible substrings of s
    for start in range(len(s)):
        for end in range(start, len(s)):
            segment = s[start:end+1]
            segment_reversed = segment[::-1]
            
            # Check if this segment matches starting at position i
            seg_len = end - start + 1
            
            # Check direct match
            if i + seg_len <= len(t) and t[i:i+seg_len] == segment:
                result.append((start + 1, end + 1))
                i += seg_len
                found = True
                break
            
            # Check reversed match
            if i + seg_len <= len(t) and t[i:i+seg_len] == segment_reversed:
                result.append((start + 1, end + 1))
                i += seg_len
                found = True
                break
        
        if found:
            break
    
    if not found:
        # Fallback to single characters (shouldn't happen due to character existence check)
        char = t[i]
        for j in range(len(s)):
            if s[j] == char:
                result.append((j + 1, j + 1))
                i += 1
                break
        else:
            print(-1)
            exit()

print(len(result))
for x, y in result:
    print(x, y)
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a string `s` that we can use
2. We can cut out any substring from `s` (continuous piece) and either use it in original order or reversed order
3. We want to build target string `t` by concatenating these pieces
4. We need to find the minimum number of such pieces and their positions

## Key Insights

1. If `t` contains a character not present in `s`, it's impossible → return -1
2. We need to find how to break `t` into substrings that exist in `s` (either originally or reversed)
3. For each position in `t`, we need to find the minimal contribution from `s`

## Algorithm Approach

1. First check if all characters in `t` exist in `s`. If not, return -1.
2. Use dynamic programming or greedy approach to find the minimum number of pieces.
3. For each position in `t`, try to extend as much as possible from `s` (both original and reversed).
4. For each possible substring in `t`, check if it matches any substring of `s` or its reverse.

Let me implement a solution:

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Try to find minimum number of pieces using greedy approach
    # For each position in t, try to match the longest possible substring
    # that appears in s or its reverse
    
    n = len(t)
    result = []
    pos = 0
    
    while pos < n:
        # Try to find the longest substring starting at pos that exists in s or reverse of s
        found = False
        
        # We'll try different lengths from current position
        for length in range(1, n - pos + 1):
            substring = t[pos:pos + length]
            
            # Check if substring exists in s (original order)
            if substring in s:
                result.append((pos + 1, pos + length))  # positions are 1-indexed
                pos += length
                found = True
                break
                
            # Check if reverse of substring exists in s
            reverse_substring = substring[::-1]
            if reverse_substring in s:
                result.append((pos + 1, pos + length))  # positions are 1-indexed (reversed)
                pos += length
                found = True
                break
        
        if not found:
            # If we couldn't find a valid piece, it means it's impossible
            # This shouldn't happen given our character check
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Output result
    print(len(result))
    for start, end in result:
        if start <= end:
            print(start, end)
        else:
            print(start, end)
```

Wait, I need to reconsider this. The problem is asking for positions within the original string `s`, not positions in the target string `t`. Let me re-read.

Actually, looking at the examples:
- Input: s="abc", t="cbaabc"
- Output: 2 pieces (3,1) and (1,3)

This means:
- First piece: from position 3 to 1 (reversed) → "cba" 
- Second piece: from position 1 to 3 (original) → "abc"

So we need to find substrings of `s` (or reversed) that match parts of `t`.

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    # Preprocess all substrings of s and their reverse
    substrings = []
    reverse_substrings = []
    
    n = len(s)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i, n):
            substr = s[i:j+1]
            substrings.append(substr)
            reverse_substrings.append(substr[::-1])
    
    # For each position in t, find the minimal piece
    result = []
    pos = 0
    
    while pos < len(t):
        found = False
        
        # Try all possible lengths from 1 to remaining length
        for length in range(1, len(t) - pos + 1):
            substring = t[pos:pos + length]
            
            # Check if substring exists in s (original)
            if substring in s:
                # Find the first occurrence of this substring in s
                start_idx = s.find(substring)
                end_idx = start_idx + len(substring) - 1
                result.append((start_idx + 1, end_idx + 1))  # 1-indexed
                pos += length
                found = True
                break
                
            # Check if reverse of substring exists in s
            reverse_substring = substring[::-1]
            if reverse_substring in s:
                start_idx = s.find(reverse_substring)
                end_idx = start_idx + len(reverse_substring) - 1
                result.append((start_idx + 1, end_idx + 1))  # 1-indexed (but this is reversed)
                pos += length
                found = True
                break
        
        if not found:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    print(len(result))
    for start, end in result:
        print(start, end)
```

Looking at the example again:

- Second piece (1,3): from position 1 to 3 → "abc"

```python
                break

                result.append((start_idx + 1, end_idx + 1))
                pos += length
                found = True
                break
        
        if not found:
            print(-1)
            return

    for x, y in result:
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a base string `s`
2. We can cut out any substring from `s` (continuous piece) and either use it as-is or in reverse
3. We want to build target string `t` by concatenating such pieces
4. We need to find the minimum number of pieces and their positions

## Key Insights

1. If `t` contains any character not present in `s`, it's impossible → return -1
2. We need to find the minimum number of substrings from `s` that can form `t`
3. Each substring can be used in original order (x ≤ y) or reversed order (x > y)
4. This is essentially a string segmentation problem where we want to minimize the number of segments

## Approach

1. First check if all characters in `t` exist in `s`. If not, return -1.
2. Use dynamic programming or greedy approach to find the minimum segmentation:
   - For each position in `t`, try to extend the current segment as much as possible
   - For each possible substring from `s`, check if it can match a prefix of the remaining `t` (either as-is or reversed)
3. Since the string length can be up to 2100, we need an efficient solution.

## Optimization Strategy

Instead of trying all possible substrings, we can:
1. Precompute all possible substrings of `s` (or their reverses) that can match prefixes of `t`
2. Use greedy matching: at each position, try to extend the current segment to match as much of the remaining `t` as possible

Actually, let me think of a simpler approach:

We can use a greedy algorithm with backtracking, but since we want minimum pieces, we should try to match longer substrings.

## Algorithm

1. Check if all characters in `t` exist in `s`. If not, return -1.
2. Use dynamic programming where `dp[i]` = minimum pieces to form `t[0:i]`
3. For each position, try every possible substring length from current position to end, and see if it matches a prefix of the remaining string (either directly or reversed)
4. Track the actual piece positions (x, y) for each piece

Wait, this might be too slow. Let me reconsider with a more efficient greedy approach.

Actually, since we're dealing with substrings of `s`, we can precompute all possible substrings of `s` and their reversed versions, then use a greedy matching from left to right.

## Simplified Approach

1. Check if all characters in `t` are in `s`
2. For each position in `t`, find the longest possible substring from `s` that can match the remaining prefix of `t` (either directly or reversed)
3. Use greedy matching to minimize the number of pieces

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    n = len(t)
    pieces = []
    pos = 0
    
    while pos < n:
        # Try to find the longest possible substring from s that can match t[pos:]
        found = False
        best_len = 0
        best_start = 0
        best_end = 0
        
        # Try all possible lengths from current pos to end
        # For each possible length, try both original and reversed
        for length in range(1, n - pos + 1):
            substr = t[pos:pos + length]
            
            # Check if substr exists in s (original order)
            if substr in s:
                # Found a match in original order
                start_pos = s.find(substr)
                if start_pos != -1:
                    best_len = length
                    best_start = start_pos
                    best_end = start_pos + length - 1
                    found = True
                    break
            
            # Check if reversed substr exists in s
            rev_substr = substr[::-1]
            if rev_substr in s:
                start_pos = s.find(rev_substr)
                if start_pos != -1:
                    best_len = length
                    best_start = start_pos
                    best_end = start_pos + length - 1
                    found = True
                    break
        
        if not found:
            print(-1)
            return
            
        pieces.append((best_start + 1, best_end + 1 if best_end >= best_start else best_start + 1))
        pos += best_len
    
    print(len(pieces))
    for start, end in pieces:
        if start <= end:
            print(start, end)
        else:
            print(start, end)

solve()
```

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

A boy named Ayrat lives on planet AMI-1511. Each inhabitant of this planet has a talent. Specifically, Ayrat loves running, moreover, just running is not enough for him. He is dreaming of making running a real art.

Unfortunately, blocks aren't freely sold to non-business customers, but Ayrat found an infinite number of coatings s. Also, he has scissors and glue. Ayrat is going to buy some coatings s, then cut out from each of them exactly one continuous piece (substring) and glue it to the end of his track coating. Moreover, he may choose to flip this block before glueing it. Ayrat want's to know the minimum number of coating s he needs to buy in order to get the coating t for his running track. Of course, he also want's to know some way to achieve the answer.


-----Input-----

First line of the input contains the string s — the coating that is present in the shop. Second line contains the string t — the coating Ayrat wants to obtain. Both strings are non-empty, consist of only small English letters and their length doesn't exceed 2100.


-----Output-----

The first line should contain the minimum needed number of coatings n or -1 if it's impossible to create the desired coating.

If x_{i} ≤ y_{i} then this piece is used in the regular order, and if x_{i} > y_{i} piece is used in the reversed order. Print the pieces in the order they should be glued to get the string t.

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a coating string `s`
2. We can cut out any substring from `s` (continuous piece) and glue it to the end of our target string `t`
3. We can optionally flip (reverse) the substring before gluing
4. We need to find the minimum number of such substrings to build string `t`
5. If impossible, return -1

## Key Insights

1. If `t` contains a character not present in `s`, it's impossible → return -1
2. We need to find the minimum number of substrings from `s` that can form `t`
3. For each position in `t`, we need to find a substring from `s` that matches either the original or reversed version
4. We want to minimize the total number of substrings used

## Algorithm Approach

1. First check if all characters in `t` exist in `s`. If not, return -1.
2. We can use dynamic programming where `dp[i]` represents the minimum number of substrings needed to form the prefix `t[0:i]`.
3. For each position, try all possible substrings from `s` that can match the current segment of `t`.
4. For each possible substring from `s`, check if it can match the current segment in original or reversed form.

However, given the constraints (up to 2100 characters), we need an efficient approach.

Alternative approach:
- Use greedy with backtracking - try to match as long as possible at each step
- For each prefix of `t`, try to find the longest substring of `s` that matches either the prefix or its reverse
- If found, use that substring and proceed

Actually, let me think of a more systematic approach:

Since we have infinite supply of `s`, we can use any substring from `s`. The key insight is that we can represent any match as either:
- A direct match of a substring from `s` with the current segment of `t`
- A reversed match of a substring from `s` with the current segment of `t`

So for each position in `t`, we need to find a substring from `s` that matches the remaining segment either directly or in reverse.

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Check if all characters in `t` exist in `s` → if not, return -1
2. Use dynamic programming where `dp[i]` = minimum number of pieces to form `t[0:i]`
3. For each position `i`, try all possible starting positions and lengths in `s`
4. Check if a substring from `s` can match `t[i-length:i]` in original or reversed form

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    t_chars = set(t)
    s_chars = set(s)
    if not t_chars.issubset(s_chars):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    n = len(t)
    # dp[i] = minimum number of pieces to form t[0:i]
    # parent[i] = (start, end) of the piece used to get to position i
    dp = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
    parent = [None] * (n + 1)
    dp[0] = 0
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        # Try all possible lengths of the last piece
        for length in range(1, i + 1):
            end_pos = i - length
            segment = t[end_pos:i]
            
            # Try to find a substring in s that matches segment (original or reversed)
            found = False
            for start in range(len(s)):
                for end in range(start, len(s)):
                    substr = s[start:end+1]
                    
                    # Check if this substring matches segment in original order
                    if substr == segment:
                        dp[i] = min(dp[i], dp[end_pos] + 1)
                        parent[i] = (start, end)
                        found = True
                        break
                    
                    # Check if this substring matches segment in reversed order
                    if substr == segment[::-1]:
                        dp[i] = min(dp[i], dp[end_pos] + 1)
                        parent[i] = (start, end)
                        found = True
                        break
                
                if found:
                    break
    
    if dp[n] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
    else:
        print(dp[n])
        # Reconstruct the solution
        result_pieces = []
        pos = n
        while pos > 0:
            start, end = parent[pos]
            result_pieces.append((start + 1, end + 1))  # Convert to 1-indexed
            pos -= (end - start + 1)
        
        # Reverse the pieces since we built them backwards
        result_pieces.reverse()
        for start, end in result_pieces:
            print(start, end)

solve()
```

Wait, this approach has a critical issue: the length of the segment we're trying to match is not being properly calculated. Let me fix this:

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    t_chars = set(t)
    s_chars = set(s)
    if not t_chars.issubset(s_chars):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    n = len(t)
    # dp[i] = minimum number of pieces to form t[0:i]
    # parent[i] = (start, end) of the piece used to get to position i
    dp = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
    parent = [None] * (n + 1)
    dp[0] = 0
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        # Try all possible lengths of the last piece
        for length in range(1, i + 1):
            segment = t[i - length:i]
            
            # Try all substrings of s
            found = False
            for start in range(len(s)):
                for end in range(start, len(s)):
                    substr = s[start:end+1]
                    
                    # Check if this substring matches segment (original order)
                    if substr == segment:
                        if dp[i - length] != float('inf'):
                            if dp[i - length] + 1 < dp[i]:
                                dp[i] = dp[i - length] + 1
                                parent[i] = (start, end)
                                found = True
                                break
                    
                    # Check if this substring matches segment (reversed order)
                    if substr == segment[::-1]:
                        if dp[i - length] != float('inf'):
                            if dp[i - length] + 1 < dp[i]:
                                dp[i] = dp[i - length] + 1
                                parent[i] = (start, end)
                                found = True
                                break
                if found:
                    break
    
    if dp[n] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
    else:
        print(dp[n])
        # Reconstruct the solution
        result_pieces = []
        pos = n
        while pos > 0:
            start, end = parent[pos]
            result_pieces.append((start + 1, end + 1))  # Convert to 1-indexed
            pos -= (end - start + 1)
        
        # Since we built from end to start, reverse to get correct order
        result_pieces.reverse()
        for start, end in result_pieces:
            print(start, end)
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a base string `s`
2. We can cut out any substring from `s` and either use it as-is or reverse it
3. We want to build target string `t` by concatenating these substrings
4. We need to find the minimum number of substrings needed, and their positions

## Key Insights

1. If `t` contains any character not present in `s`, it's impossible → return -1
2. We need to find how to build `t` from substrings of `s`
3. Each substring can be in original order (x ≤ y) or reversed order (x > y)
4. We want to minimize the number of substrings

## Algorithm Approach

This is essentially a DP problem where we try to build `t` character by character.

For each position in `t`, we need to check:
- Can we extend the current prefix by a substring from `s`?
- What are the valid substrings we can use from `s`?

However, a more practical approach:
1. First check if all characters in `t` exist in `s`
2. Use dynamic programming where `dp[i]` represents the minimum number of substrings needed to form `t[0:i]`
3. For each position, try all possible substrings from `s` that could extend the current string

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Impossibility check**: If any character in `t` is not in `s`, return -1
2. **String matching**: We need to find the minimum number of pieces
3. **DP approach**: For each position in `t`, try all possible substrings of `s` that can match the next part

Actually, let me think of a simpler greedy approach:
- We can try to match as long a substring as possible from `s`
- For each position in `t`, we check what substrings of `s` can match the remaining part

## Better approach - DP with state

Let `dp[i]` = minimum number of pieces to form `t[0:i]`

For each state, we try all possible substrings from `s` that can match a suffix of `t[i:]`.

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    n = len(t)
    m = len(s)
    
    # dp[i] = minimum number of pieces to form t[0:i]
    # parent[i] = (start, end) of the last piece that formed t[0:i]
    dp = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
    parent = [-1] * (n + 1)
    
    dp[0] = 0
    
    for i in range(1, n + 1):
        # Try all possible substrings from s
        for j in range(i, n + 1):  # we want to extend from position i to j-1
            # Actually, we need to try all possible substrings of s that can match t[i:j]
            # But we need to check if t[i:j] can be formed by some substring of s
            # either in normal or reversed order
            
            # Try all possible substring lengths
            for length in range(1, j - i + 1):
                substr = t[i:i + length]
                
                # Check if substr can be formed by some substring of s
                found = False
                for start in range(m):
                    # Try original order
                    if s[start:start + length] == substr:
                        dp[j] = min(dp[j], dp[i] + 1)
                        parent[j] = (start, start + length - 1)
                        found = True
                        break
                    
                    # Try reversed order
                    if s[start:start + length][::-1] == substr:
                        dp[j] = min(dp[j], dp[i] + 1)
                        parent[j] = (start, start + length - 1)
                        found = True
                        break
                
                if found:
                    break
    
    if dp[n] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Reconstruct the answer
    result = []
    pos = n
    while pos > 0:
        start, end = parent[pos]
        result.append((start + 1, end + 1))  # 1-indexed
        pos -= (end - start + 1)
    
    result.reverse()
    print(dp[n])
    for start, end in result:
        print(start, end)
```

## Correct Approach

We need to build `t` from substrings of `s`. For each position, we try to extend with a substring from `s`.

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    # Check if all characters in t exist in s
    s_set = set(s)
    for char in t:
        if char not in s_set:
            print(-1)
            return
    
    n = len(t)
    m = len(s)
    
    # dp[i] = minimum number of pieces to form t[0:i]
    dp = [float('inf')] * (n + 1)
    parent = [-1] * (n + 1)  # parent[i] = (start, end, next_pos) for the piece that forms t[i:next_pos]
    
    dp[0] = 0
    
    for i in range(n):
        # Try to extend from position i to j
        for j in range(i + 1, n + 1):
            # We want to form t[i:j] using one piece from s
            substr = t[i:j]
            
            # Check if substr can be formed by some substring of s in original order
            found = False
            for start in range(m - len(substr) + 1):
                if s[start:start + len(substr)] == substr:
                    if dp[i] != float('inf'):
                        if dp[j] > dp[i] + 1:
                            dp[j] = dp[i] + 1
                            parent[j] = (start, start + len(substr) - 1, i)
                    found = True
                    break
            
            # Check if substr can be formed by some substring of s in reversed order
            if not found:
                for start in range(m - len(substr) + 1):
                    if s[start:start + len(substr)][::-1] == substr:
                        if dp[i] != float('inf'):
                            if dp[j] > dp[i] + 1:
                                dp[j] = dp[i] + 1
                                parent[j] = (start, start + len(substr) - 1, i)
                        found = True
                        break
    
    if dp[n] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Reconstruct the solution
    result = []
    pos = n
    while pos > 0:
        start, end, prev_pos = parent[pos]
        # The piece covers from start to end (0-indexed) in string s
        # This corresponds to indices (start + 1, end + 1) in 1-indexed
        result.append((start + 1, end + 1))
        pos = prev_pos
    
    result.reverse()
    print(dp[n])
    for start, end in result:
        print(start, end)
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an infinite supply of a coating string `s`
2. We want to create target string `t` by cutting substrings from `s` and either using them as-is or reversed
3. We need to find the minimum number of substrings to use and their positions (start, end indices)
4. If impossible, return -1

## Key Insights

1. Since we have infinite copies of string `s`, we can extract any substring from `s` (or any cyclic shift of `s`)
2. For each character in `t`, we need to find a substring from `s` that matches it (either directly or in reverse)
3. We want to minimize the total number of substrings used

## Algorithm

1. First, check if it's possible to create `t` from `s`:
   - For each character in `t`, we need to find a substring in `s` that can match it
   - A substring from `s` can match a character pattern in two ways: original order or reversed

2. For each position in `t`, we need to find a valid substring from `s` that can produce that character pattern.

3. Use dynamic programming or greedy approach to minimize the number of substrings.

4. Since we can use any substring from `s` (anywhere in any copy), we need to check if each character in `t` appears in `s` or its reverse.

## Refined Approach

1. First check if the problem is solvable:
   - For each character in `t`, we need to find if it appears in `s` or in the reverse of `s`
   - Actually, we need to match entire contiguous substrings, not individual characters

2. Actually, we need to match the entire string `t` using consecutive substrings from `s` (with reversal option).

3. We can use a greedy approach:
   - For each position in `t`, try to match as many characters as possible from `s` (either in original or reversed order)
   - Keep track of the minimum number of pieces needed

Example 2: s="aaabrytaaa", t="ayrat"
- "a" from position 1 to 1 (original)

- "at" from position 8 to 7 (reversed, so "ta" becomes "at")

## Solution Strategy

```python
def solve():
    s = input().strip()
    t = input().strip()
    
    n_s, n_t = len(s), len(t)
    
    # Check if any character in t is not present in s or reverse(s)
    s_rev = s[::-1]
    all_chars = set(s + s_rev)
    t_chars = set(t)
    
    if not t_chars <= all_chars:
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # We need to find minimum number of substrings from s (with reversal) to form t
    # Use DP: dp[i] = (min_pieces, list_of_intervals) for t[0:i]
    
    # For each position in t, we can try to match a substring from s
    # with various start and end positions
    
    # dp[i] = minimum number of pieces to form t[0:i]
    # We'll store the actual intervals as well
    
    dp = [float('inf')] * (n_t + 1)
    prev = [None] * (n_t + 1)  # stores (start, end) for each position
    
    dp[0] = 0
    
    for i in range(1, n_t + 1):
        # Try all possible end positions j where we can take a substring of t[0:j]
        # and match it with some substring of s or reverse(s)
        for j in range(i):
            if dp[j] == float('inf'):
                continue
                
            # Try all possible substring lengths from j to i-1
            for length in range(1, i - j + 1):
                substring_t = t[j:i]
                match_found = False
                
                # Try to find a substring in s that matches substring_t
                # or reverse of some substring in s that matches substring_t
                found = False
                
                # Try every possible start in s
                for start in range(n_s):
                    # Original order
                    substr_s = s[start:start + length]
                    if substr_s == substring_t:
                        # Found in original order
                        # We need to find the start and end positions in the original s
                        # with offset from beginning
                        # But we need to find where this substring is in s
                        # Actually, we just need to remember the indices
                        end = start + length - 1
                        if end >= start:
                            # We found a valid interval
                            new_pieces = dp[j] + 1
                            if new_pieces < dp[i]:
                                dp[i] = new_pieces
                                prev[i] = (start + 1, end + 1)  # 1-indexed
                                found = True
                                break
                    # Try reversed order
                    substr_s_rev = s[start:start + length][::-1]
                    if substr_s_rev == substring_t:
                        # Found in reversed order
                        end = start + length - 1
                        if end >= start:
                            new_pieces = dp[j] + 1
                            if new_pieces < dp[i]:
                                dp[i] = new_pieces
                                prev[i] = (start + 1, end + 1)  # 1-indexed
                                found = True
                                break
                
                if found:
                    break
        
        if dp[i] == float('inf'):
            # Try all possible lengths from 1 to i
            # Just try to find any match
            for length in range(1, i + 1):
                substring_t = t[i - length:i]
                match_found = False
                
                for start in range(n_s):
                    substr_s = s[start:start + length]
                    if substr_s == substring_t:
                        new_pieces = dp[i - length] + 1
                        if new_pieces < dp[i]:
                            dp[i] = new_pieces
                            prev[i] = (start + 1, start + length)
                            match_found = True
                            break
                    substr_s_rev = s[start:start + length][::-1]
                    if substr_s_rev == substring_t:
                        new_pieces = dp[i - length] + 1
                        if new_pieces < dp[i]:
                            dp[i] = new_pieces
                            prev[i] = (start + 1, start + length)
                            match_found = True
                            break
                if match_found:
                    break
    
    if dp[n_t] == float('inf'):
        print(-1)
        return
    
    # Reconstruct the solution
    result = []
    pos = n_t
    while pos > 0:
        start, end = prev[pos]
        result.append((start, end))
        pos -= end - start + 1
    
    # Reverse to get correct order
    result.reverse()
    
    print(dp[n_t])
    for start, end in result:
        if start <= end:
            print(start, end)
        else:
            print(start, end)
```

```python

t = input().strip()

                # Try original: s[start:start+length]
                if start + length <= n_s and s[start:start+length] == sub_t:
                    dp[i] = min(dp[i], dp[j] + 1)
                    prev[i] = (start + 1, start + length)
                    found = True
                    break
                # Try reversed: reverse of s[start:start+length] should equal sub_t
                if start + length <= n_s and s[start:start+length][::-1] == sub_t:
                    dp[i] = min(dp[i], dp[j] + 1)
                    prev[i] = (start + 1, start + length)  # actually we need to adjust for reversal
                    found = True
                    break
            
            if found:
                break

if dp[n_t] == float('inf'):
    print(-1)
else:
    print(dp[n_t])
    result = []
    pos = n_t
    while pos > 0:
        start, end = prev[pos]
        result.append((start, end))
        pos -= end - start + 1
    result.reverse()
    for start, end in result:
        print(start, end)
```

This still has issues with the reversal case. For reversal, if we're using reversed order, we need to output the original indices but indicate reversal with x > y.

```python
                        prev[i] = (start + 1, end)  # 1-indexed
                        found = True
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

You are now examining a unique method to sort a sequence of numbers in increasing order. The method only allows swapping of two numbers that have a common prime factor. For example, a sequence [6, 4, 2, 3, 7] can be sorted using the following steps.
Step 0: 6 4 2 3 7 (given sequence)
Step 1: 2 4 6 3 7 (elements 6 and 2 swapped)
Step 2: 2 6 4 3 7 (elements 4 and 6 swapped)
Step 3: 2 3 4 6 7 (elements 6 and 3 swapped)


Depending on the nature of the sequence, however, this approach may fail to complete the sorting. You have given a name "Coprime sort" to this approach and are now examining if a given sequence is coprime-sortable.

Make a program to determine if a given sequence can be sorted in increasing order by iterating an arbitrary number of swapping operations of two elements that have a common prime number.



Input

The input is given in the following format.


$N$
$a_1$ $a_2$ $...$ $a_N$


The first line provides the number of elements included in the sequence $N$ ($2 \leq N \leq 10^5$). The second line provides an array of integers $a_i$ ($2 \leq a_i \leq 10^5$) that constitute the sequence.

Output

Output "1" if the sequence is coprime-sortable in increasing order, or "0" otherwise.

Examples

Input

5
6 4 2 3 7


Output

1


Input

7
2 9 6 5 6 7 3


Output

0

The input will be given via stdin and the output should be printed to stdout by your code.

Now solve the problem by providing the code.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

I'll solve this step-by-step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to determine if a sequence can be sorted in increasing order by swapping elements that have a common prime factor.
2. Two elements can be swapped if they share at least one prime factor.
3. The question is whether we can eventually sort the sequence using such swaps.

## Key Insights

1. If two elements share a prime factor, they can be swapped directly.
2. If we can swap elements that share prime factors, we can essentially move elements around within "connected components" formed by prime factors.
3. Elements that can reach each other through a chain of swaps form a connected component.
4. For the sequence to be sortable, all elements in each connected component must be able to reach their correct positions in the sorted sequence.

## Analysis

Think about it this way:
- Elements that share prime factors belong to the same "group"
- Elements in the same group can be freely rearranged among themselves
- For the sequence to be sortable, each group must contain exactly the elements that belong to that group in the sorted sequence

So the approach:
1. For each element, find its prime factors
2. Group elements by their prime factors (elements sharing any prime factor are in the same group)
3. For each group, check if the multiset of values in that group in the original sequence matches the multiset of values in that group in the sorted sequence

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Find prime factors for each number
2. Group indices by their prime factors
3. Sort the original array
4. For each group, compare the frequency of elements in original vs sorted sequence

```python
def get_prime_factors(n):
    factors = set()
    d = 2
    while d * d <= n:
        while n % d == 0:
            factors.add(d)
            n //= d
        d += 1
    if n > 1:
        factors.add(n)
    return factors

def solve():
    n = int(input())
    a = list(map(int, input().split()))
    
    # Create a mapping from each prime factor to the indices that contain elements with that prime factor
    prime_to_indices = {}
    
    # For each element, find its prime factors and add to prime_to_indices
    for i, num in enumerate(a):
        factors = get_prime_factors(num)
        for factor in factors:
            if factor not in prime_to_indices:
                prime_to_indices[factor] = []
            prime_to_indices[factor].append(i)
    
    # Create sorted version of the array
    sorted_a = sorted(a)
    
    # For each prime factor group, check if the multiset of values in the original array 
    # matches the multiset of values in the sorted array for that group
    for prime, indices in prime_to_indices.items():
        # Get values at these indices in original array
        original_values = [a[i] for i in indices]
        # Get values at these indices in sorted array
        sorted_values = [sorted_a[i] for i in indices]
        
        # Check if both multisets are identical
        if sorted(original_values) != sorted(sorted_values):
            return 0
    
    return 1

print(solve())
```

Wait, I need to reconsider this. The above approach might not work correctly because we're checking each prime factor group independently.

Let me think again. The key insight is that elements that can be swapped (share a prime factor) form connected components. Within each connected component, we can rearrange elements arbitrarily. So for the sequence to be sortable, the multiset of elements in each connected component must match the multiset of elements in the corresponding positions after sorting.

Actually, let me refine this:

For a sequence to be coprime-sortable:
1. Elements that share a prime factor can be swapped
2. This creates connected components of positions
3. Within each connected component, we can rearrange elements arbitrarily
4. Therefore, the frequency of each value in each connected component must match in both original and sorted arrays

But I need to be more careful about how the components are formed.

Actually, the connected components are formed by elements that can be reached through prime factors. Two elements are in the same component if there's a chain of elements that share prime factors.

A simpler approach: Two elements can be swapped if they share a prime factor. This means all elements with a common prime factor can be rearranged among themselves. So the necessary and sufficient condition is that for each prime p, the multiset of values in the positions that contain numbers divisible by p must be the same in the original and sorted arrays.

Wait, that's not quite right either.

Let me go back to the fundamental insight: if elements can be freely rearranged within their connected components, then for the sequence to be sortable, each connected component must contain exactly the same elements (same multiset) as in the target sorted sequence.

The connected components are formed by elements that share prime factors. So:

1. Find all prime factors of each element
2. For each prime factor, create a group of indices containing elements with that prime factor
3. For each group, check if the multiset of values in original array at those indices matches the multiset of values in sorted array at those indices

```python
def get_prime_factors(n):
    factors = set()
    d = 2
    while d * d <= n:
        while n % d == 0:
            factors.add(d)
            n //= d
        d += 1
    if n > 1:
        factors.add(n)
    return factors

n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Create a mapping from each prime factor to the indices that contain elements with that prime factor
prime_to_indices = {}

# For each element, find its prime factors and add to prime_to_indices
for i, num in enumerate(a):
    factors = get_prime_factors(num)
    for factor in factors:
        if factor not in prime_to_indices:
            prime_to_indices[factor] = []
        prime_to_indices[factor].append(i)

# Create sorted version of the array
sorted_a = sorted(a)

# For each prime factor group, check if the multiset of values in the original array 
# matches the multiset of values in the sorted array for that group
for prime, indices in prime_to_indices.items():
    # Get values at these indices in original array
    original_values = [a[i] for i in indices]
    # Get values at these indices in sorted array
    sorted_values = [sorted_a[i] for i in indices]
    
    # Check if both multisets are identical
    if sorted(original_values) != sorted(sorted_values):
        print(0)
        exit()

print(1)
```

def get_prime_factors(n):
    factors = set()
    d = 2
    while d * d <= n:
        while n % d == 0:
            factors.add(d)
            n //= d
        d += 1
    if n > 1:
        factors.add(n)
    return factors

n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# For each element, find its prime factors and add to prime_to_indices

    factors = get_prime_factors(num)
    for factor in factors:
        if factor not in prime_to_indices:
            prime_to_indices[factor] = []
        prime_to_indices[factor].append(i)

# Create sorted version of the array
sorted_a = sorted(a)

# matches the multiset of values in the sorted array for that group
for prime, indices in prime_to_indices.items():
    # Get values at these indices in original array
    original_values = [a[i] for i in indices]
    # Get values at these indices in sorted array
    sorted_values = [sorted_a[i] for i in indices]
    
    # Check if both multisets are identical
    if sorted(original_values) != sorted(sorted_values):

        exit()

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

You are now examining a unique method to sort a sequence of numbers in increasing order. The method only allows swapping of two numbers that have a common prime factor. For example, a sequence [6, 4, 2, 3, 7] can be sorted using the following steps.
Step 0: 6 4 2 3 7 (given sequence)
Step 1: 2 4 6 3 7 (elements 6 and 2 swapped)
Step 2: 2 6 4 3 7 (elements 4 and 6 swapped)
Step 3: 2 3 4 6 7 (elements 6 and 3 swapped)


Depending on the nature of the sequence, however, this approach may fail to complete the sorting. You have given a name "Coprime sort" to this approach and are now examining if a given sequence is coprime-sortable.

Make a program to determine if a given sequence can be sorted in increasing order by iterating an arbitrary number of swapping operations of two elements that have a common prime number.



Input

The input is given in the following format.


$N$
$a_1$ $a_2$ $...$ $a_N$


The first line provides the number of elements included in the sequence $N$ ($2 \leq N \leq 10^5$). The second line provides an array of integers $a_i$ ($2 \leq a_i \leq 10^5$) that constitute the sequence.

Output

Output "1" if the sequence is coprime-sortable in increasing order, or "0" otherwise.

Examples

Input

5
6 4 2 3 7


Output

1


Input

7
2 9 6 5 6 7 3


Output

0

The input will be given via stdin and the output should be printed to stdout by your code.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to determine if a sequence can be sorted in increasing order by swapping elements that have a common prime factor
2. Two elements can be swapped if they share at least one prime factor
3. We need to output 1 if coprime-sortable, 0 otherwise

## Key Insight

This is essentially a graph connectivity problem:
- Each number in the array can be connected to other numbers that share at least one prime factor
- Elements within the same connected component can be freely rearranged (through a series of swaps)
- For the sequence to be sortable, all elements that need to reach their final positions must be in the same connected component

## Algorithm Steps

1. **Prime factorization**: Find all prime factors of each number in the array
2. **Group by prime factors**: Elements that share a prime factor form a connected component
3. **Check connectivity**: For the sequence to be sortable, each element must be in a component that can reach its target position

## Detailed Approach

The key insight is that we can consider the elements as being in connected components where two elements are in the same component if they share at least one prime factor.

For the sequence to be coprime-sortable:
- Elements in the same connected component can be rearranged freely
- So, we need to check if elements that need to be in certain positions can be moved there through valid swaps

However, there's a simpler way: 
- Elements that don't share any prime factors with others might be "isolated"
- But more importantly, if elements need to move to positions where no prime factor is shared with other elements that need to be there, it might not be possible

Actually, the core insight is:
- All elements that are connected through shared prime factors can be freely rearranged within their component
- For the sequence to be sortable, within each connected component, the elements must be able to reach their target positions

But there's an even simpler insight: if we consider the prime factors, elements that share a prime factor can be swapped. So we can think of this as: build a graph where each number is a node, and there's an edge between two numbers if they share a prime factor. Then, elements within connected components can be freely rearranged.

The condition for sortability is that for each connected component, the multiset of elements in that component must contain exactly the elements that need to be in that component when sorted.

## Implementation Steps

1. Find prime factors of each number
2. Build connected components using union-find or DFS
3. For each connected component, collect all elements in it
4. Sort these elements and compare with the sorted version of the original array, but only considering the positions that belong to that component
